commission. – mr president, undoubtedly the media play a crucial role in subjecting the exercise of public and private powers to permanent scrutiny. media pluralism is therefore of the utmost importance and lies at the very heart of the way our democratic societies operate. indeed it is one of the core values underlying the european union, as enshrined in article 11(2) of the charter of fundamental rights, which refers explicitly to protecting the pluralism of the media. on this subject, i can only endorse what you say regarding the situation in the media sector following developments over the past ten years. i would add that while audiences may be focused on a small number of programmes, european citizens have, nonetheless, a far wider choice in their viewing, thanks to a range of technologies. in this new setting, the commission has made use of the instruments at its disposal. in applying competition law it upholds freedom of competition in the media, particularly in innovative media services such as the internet and umts. moreover, it applies the mergers regulation which serves to control the phenomenon of media concentration from the economic angle. in addition, the community legislator, on the basis of commission proposals, adopted the television without frontiers directive, the satellite and cable directive and the conditional access directive, which address various aspects of cross-border television broadcasting. the legislative framework was further supplemented by the e-commerce directive, adopted in 2000, the objective of which is to facilitate cross-border provision of new interactive media services distributed on a point-to-point basis. finally, the commission proposal for a common regulatory framework for liberalising electronic communications networks and services was adopted in march this year. one of its main aims is to open up access to electronic communications networks, including those which carry broadcast content. to this end, it provides that operators who are identified as having a strong market position may have to meet ex-ante obligations on transparency, non-discrimination, accounting separation and access to or use of specific facilities. the commission monitors closely the transposition of these directives in the applicant countries as well as in the member states, ensuring that national media legislation remains consistent with community law. while existing community law instruments undoubtedly have positive repercussions on the protection of pluralism, it has to be said that their purpose is to ensure a certain economic balance between players in the context of the internal market. these instruments directly affect the media sector as an area of economic activity and not – or at least only very indirectly – as a vehicle for delivering information to the citizen. this situation stems from the fact that even though pluralism is a fundamental freedom recognised by the eu, it is not one of the tasks assigned to the european community under article 2 of the ec treaty. this is borne out by article 21 of the mergers regulation, which allows member states to forbid a merger in the interests of protecting pluralism even where it has been approved by the commission. consequently, any new initiative based on internal market freedoms would encounter the same limitation as the instruments i have mentioned and would not ensure the protection of pluralism per se. the question also arises whether such an initiative could be justified by reference to existing treaty provisions. the commission is not aware of any concrete obstacles to exercise of the fundamental freedoms of the ec treaty, particularly the right of establishment or freedom to provide services within the meaning of articles 43 and 49, being encountered in the media sector because of national measures designed to protect pluralism per se. moreover, any initiative would have to be aimed at eliminating significant distortion of competition. under court of justice case-law, these are the tests for community action on the basis of article 95, involving measures to improve the operation of the single market. we also have to consider whether such an initiative would comply with the principle of subsidiarity, since the purpose of legislative proposals based on internal market freedoms must in any event be to establish obstacles to the cross-border provision of services, not to regulate for specific situations in member states. as to whether it is desirable for the european convention to provide for the protection of pluralism in general at eu level, i would recall that the commission, like parliament, believes the charter of fundamental rights should be incorporated into the basic treaty and constitutionally entrenched. finally, i close with the reminder that the council of europe exists, among other things, to preserve cultural pluralism and media diversity. closer cooperation with that institution should also be envisaged in order to enhance the protection of pluralism in europe.
- mr president, one of the essential principles of a well-functioning democratic society is freedom of expression and access to information. in this house we have repeatedly stressed the importance of openness and transparency. the citizen must have access to information concerning his life so that he can participate in the democratic debate and make his choice. the media play a central role in this process. if they don't work, democracy doesn't work. today, there are trends in the media sector that give cause for concern. the consolidation we have seen may lead to a reduction in media pluralism. we are fighting monopolies because the supply of goods is being reduced, and the resulting decline in wealth, and the same applies to the media market. monopolies undermine democratic pluralism and lead to a decline in democratic welfare. in the past, party-political media, such as newspapers, could only exercise political censorship within their own media. they had no control over what other media outlets were broadcasting. the threat we face today is a strong consolidation of potentials. it could lead to certain views not being represented at all in any media. as elected legislators, we face a delicate balancing act. what means can we take to ensure a free and diverse media without at the same time neglecting another cardinal principle of a democratic society - the sharing of power? we want to establish rules of the game that ensure a level playing field without compromising the content. it is necessary that we in this house live up to our democratic responsibilities and ensure that the media live up to theirs.
commission. – madam president, i read with attention the report adopted by the committee on fisheries on our action plan on environmental integration. we are happy to note that our views are rather more convergent on this issue of environmental integration than on other reform aspects. we clearly share the same goals in respect of such important fields of action suggested in the report as environmental protection, improvement of research, including the integration of fishermen's experience, specific measures for specific problems, the implementation of an ecosystem approach, aids to modernisation, industrial fisheries, sustainable aquaculture and so on. in fact the commission has already started or planned several initiatives to respond to these requirements. allow me to outline some of them. we have requested the ices to study in 2003 the possibilities for operational procedures to integrate fishermen's experience and knowledge in the assessment and advisory process. we agree that specific situations require specific measures. we do not apply the same combinations of management instruments in the baltic as in the north sea or the mediterranean. we do not intend, either, to set the same targets for different fleets. to achieve the targets set out in johannesburg, we are in the process of implementing the bio-diversity action plan with a view to halting bio-diversity decline by 2010 and it is through the reformed cfp that we intend to rebuild stocks sooner than 2015. we have recently co-financed an fao expert meeting to develop operational guidelines to implement an ecosystem-based approach to fisheries management. in our reform proposals we have excluded the mandatory scrapping of vessels and given more emphasis to multiannual plans using flexible instruments such as tacs, effort regulation and technical measures as main regulatory elements. we have proposed to maintain aid to modernisation of vessels for reasons of security, working conditions and hygiene, provided that there is no increased fishing efficiency in order to prevent increases in fishing pressure. we are promoting the elaboration by the main stakeholders of a european code of responsible fishing practice. the first drafts are already under discussion in the context of the advisory committee for fisheries and aquaculture. we have requested that the ices study the ecological consequences of industrial fisheries and the effects on non-fishery activities, as well as the threats, including global warming, to commercial stocks. the commission has initiated debate to promote a coordinated protection of the marine environment. the recent communication on a strategy to protect and conserve the marine environment gives the main elements of the policy advocated for a holistic treatment of the problem. we have addressed the erika oil spill by completing most elements of the package, namely the rules on port state control, certification and checking of security measures and bringing forward the phasing out of single hull tankers. the creation of an eu compensation fund for oil spills is expected to be finalised by 2003. we are constantly putting as much pressure as we can on member states for a better implementation of the habitats directive in the marine environment as completely and as soon as possible. finally, as you have already had the opportunity to study our communication on sustainable aquaculture, you may have noticed how much our intended policy in the area takes into account environmental protection requirements. as far as fisheries agreements are concerned, you will soon have an opportunity to confirm that the commission shares your environmental concerns. the announced communication on partnership agreements will be presented shortly. generally speaking, the commission is on your side regarding the main resolutions of the report. however, i wish to comment on several statements in the explanatory memorandum. firstly, while i recognise the shortcomings of scientific advice, i strongly disagree that this is the main problem of fisheries management. we all know that the main problem is too many fishermen chasing too few fish. secondly, the rapporteur states that it is the commission is pursuing a policy of capacity reduction. as i have said before on many occasions, this is simply not correct. it is not correct, either, that the commission has proposed the abolition of public aid for modernisation of the fleet. finally, i would not agree that industrial fishing has a much greater environmental impact per euro added value than fisheries for human consumption, mainly because i find it odd to measure environmental impact against monetary units, but also because the environmental impact of this kind of fishing has yet to be determined. to sum up, i do not agree totally with the form but will support the substance of the report and in particular the motion for a resolution.

- mr president, we are at a crucial juncture in the history of europe. i think we all felt a pleasant rush during this morning's meeting. from 1 may 2004, the community will comprise no less than 25 countries. almost all of europe is now being brought together in a community. ten applicant countries have substantially completed their preparations for membership. it has been a demanding and often painful process for these countries. they still need to build up the administrative and judicial capacity to implement and comply with eu legislation in practice, particularly in the fields of agriculture, food and the environment. this applies also at regional and local level. i think it is important, among other things, that the requirements for integrating environmental and health protection are actually being met in all sectors. this is particularly true in the field of agriculture and infrastructure development. this is a requirement which applies up to the accession of the countries, but certainly also to a very great extent thereafter. within the framework laid down most recently at the brussels summit in october, the terms of accession must now be definitively agreed for each new member state. the basic requirements for these negotiations will be a high degree of flexibility, solidarity with the applicant countries and solidarity between the member states. we must ensure that the new member states enjoy fair conditions of competition so that their accession to the eu can contribute positively to the welfare of the countries. i say this because only a positive and convincing vote by the citizens in the forthcoming referendums will put the important enlargement in the right perspective. i am convinced that the danish presidency and commissioner verheugen have the flexibility and the spirit of solidarity which will enable the final decision on the admission of each country to the union to be taken in copenhagen, if not on 12 or 13 december.

- mr president, if you read the commission's legislative programme carefully, you will suddenly come across a european army, the rapid reaction force. it is to be established under the so-called rubber clause, the famous article 235, now article 308, which remains unchanged in the treaty of nice. this shows that there are no limits to the eu's involvement in the member states. with so many new countries joining the eu next year, it would be better for us all to focus on consolidating and simplifying existing legislation rather than continuing to build the brussels legal pyramid bigger and bigger. the commission should be allowed to make new proposals only on condition that it abolishes the old legislation. all new proposals should be presented in their legal context, so that it is possible to compare new legislative proposals with existing legislation. no new proposal may be submitted without being accompanied by proposals on how to consolidate existing legislation in this area. such an approach would make it possible to reduce the existing body of eu law of over 85,000 pages to perhaps 25,000 pages. after that exercise, we should try to simplify binding legislation into non-binding recommendations, regulations can be changed into framework legislation, total harmonisation directives can be changed into minimum harmonisation, and uniform rules can be changed into voluntary rules. attempts to create a federal state with an ever-growing body of legislation could be replaced by a decentralized federation of states that allows the member states much more freedom. this is our vision of how the commission's legislative programme should be designed. we want a europe of democracies based on diversity because we believe that people will be happier if they can decide for themselves as much as possible without interference and free from detailed rules drawn up by bureaucrats and lobbyists in brussels.

- mr president, if a small danish limited company with 100 employees submitted an annual report on which the state auditor issued a statement emphasizing that most of the reservations were due to recurrence from year to year; and if it was found that the weaknesses were largely due to the company's accounting system not providing sufficient assurance of a complete accounting of all elements of the company's assets; and if it went on to write that there were problems with the control and safety systems, that the data in the system was not complete, and went on to say that it was worrying that the company had not prioritised a remedying of these deficiencies sufficiently and that it had not made sufficient considerations in the field or sufficient resources to resolve the problems within a reasonable time horizon. if it was ultimately concluded that no declaration could be made as to whether the works, purchases or services underlying the payments to the local beneficiaries had actually been carried out. yes, if that happened, the board would, for one, refuse to discharge the executive. then they would fire the management. the tax authorities would then come and seize all the company's accounting documents for critical analysis. then the company would be taxed in the amount of the undocumented expenditure and finally it would be subject to criminal liability. but that is not the case in the eu. this is the only way we can deal with our private companies. here the commission is trying by all means to silence the critics, as has just happened with the dismissed director of accounts marta andreasen, and when parliament wants to bury her criticism, the majority in the conference of presidents prevents us from holding a hearing. if the criticism were unjustified, as the commission claims, then we would have it clarified and then everything would be fine. but the commission knows, and the court of auditors confirms, that the criticism is justified and must therefore not be made public. instead, this parliament chooses to turn a blind eye. it is called discharge, because otherwise the citizens might find out that there is no order in the financial affairs. i think it is extremely unwise to continue this policy of silence and i do not think that in the long run we will be able to avoid the scandal of the european union not being able to manage the money that our taxpayers hand over to us. i find the account very informative. the problem is that we dare not put action behind the criticism.

- mr president, the copenhagen summit will be historic. ten countries will be able to join from 2004, two countries from 2007 and turkey will presumably be given a date for when to meet again. europe is growing together. that's good. but the eu membership now being negotiated is not what the applicant countries will receive. they can say yes to the european union, but they will be granted the status of a state in a new federal state by next christmas, when the rome summit will have buried the treaty of rome in favour of the rome constitution. the new constitution is scheduled to be finalized before the new countries join and are given veto power over treaty changes. it is not fair, as bertel haarder also said. once the applicant countries have signed the membership agreements and when they are approved here in parliament in march or april, they should be offered equal status with the present member states and have their legal representation in all bodies. if membership is rejected in a referendum, the representation can be terminated in the same way as it was when norway voted against membership of the community and therefore had to find an ambassador job for the norwegian commissioner. if the referendums confirm membership, then that will be a reality and countries should have the same veto over treaty changes as the current member states. they're not to be admitted as b-members, they're to be equal from day one. i also think we could be more accommodating in terms of financing their eu membership. when a rich country like denmark joined in 1973, we actually paid only 20% quota the first year, 40% the next, 60% the third and 80% the fourth, until after five years we reached full quota. why can't we adopt a similar system of exclusion or grant quota exemptions as long as the countries are so far from our wealth? - mr president, i should like to thank the rapporteur for his tireless enthusiasm for the development of europe and for a very detailed and fine report. i would like to point out two points on which i disagree. the first point concerns the procedure for revising the treaties. i fully agree that it is clear from the work of the convention that we must use the convention method in future for treaty amendments. at least that's what we've learned from this process. however, i must say that i strongly disagree with the rapporteur's proposal to change the ratification procedure itself. i do not think it is in keeping with the spirit of our community to deprive the member states of control over the development of the union. i am afraid that such a procedure will in itself prevent progress in our europe, because the people will be very sceptical about giving europe more powers, for example, if they do not feel that they are in control of what is happening. i therefore believe that we must recognize that europe is built on the citizens and the peoples, but that the basic constituent units of our union are the member states, and that this must of course be reflected in our ratification procedure. we should simply maintain the procedure we have at present. the second point concerns the location of the social dialogue in the community. the rapporteur is trying to get an approval procedure from the council and the european parliament in place once the social partners have reached a proposal. i believe that such an approach would create an inappropriate third party in the social dialogue. the social dialogue is designed to ensure that the two sides of the labour market enter into binding agreements with each other and that no unrelated parties - in this case legislators - are involved in such agreements. if we are serious about starting a social dialogue and perhaps eventually creating real european agreements, we must recognize that the social partners themselves must negotiate and that the legislators must stay out of it.

the commission. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i have to say that i am very pleased with the way the commission has been working. the commission has today adopted a package of financial reform which marks a crucial stage in the reform efforts of this commission. the legislative framework for the new financial regulation is being completed: the commission adopted the implementing regulation on the financial regulation and the rules on the recovery of claims. with a great deal of effort from all parties involved, including in particular the european parliament, the objective of a new, modern budgetary law for the european union by 1 january 2003 has been achieved. this new budgetary law will better protect the financial interests of the community - i give examples of the guarantees to be provided by beneficiaries in the case of advance payments, the obligations on auditors' wills of contractors or the possibility for the commission to exclude from tendering procedures where irregularities have been detected in the past by the contractor. this new budgetary law will also make budget implementation more transparent and coherent. secondly, the commission has made an overall assessment of the state of preparation of the commission services for the implementation of this new budgetary law. it notes that considerable progress has been made in the transfer of the old ex ante controls to the internal control procedures of the services. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the programme and will continue to monitor the implementation of the programme. the horizontal services will provide targeted support. this creates the conditions for the closure of the central financial control at the end of this financial year and the dissolution of the remaining parts of the directorate-general for financial control by spring 2003. the staff made available will be mainly allocated to the services to supplement the human resources already added to the services over the last three years for financial reform. the commission's managing bodies will thus be fully responsible for the implementation of the budget, including the necessary control measures. the commission has also adopted today a communication on the modernisation of the community's accounting system. this communication is divided into three parts. first, the reform of accounting rules; second, the adaptation of the supporting information and technology system; and third, the architecture of project management. we have undertaken on behalf of the commission to present these measures and the timetable to the european parliament and the court of auditors by the end of this year. the commission is thus taking another important step in the long-term strategy for modernising accounting, which it launched in 2000. in 2000, the commission presented a study which led it to propose that the financial regulation adopt new accounting rules based on accrual accounting. this communication now sets out step by step the necessary actions needed to have the modern system in place by 2005. the future system of accounting will be a dual system. the budgetary accounts will be drawn up in accordance with the traditional cash accounting for revenue and expenditure of the budget, as required by the budgetary authority for the control of the implementation of the budget, i.e. actual expenditure and actual revenue will be compared with the estimates as decided in the budget. the accrual accounting will also have to integrate the asset accounting for the financial accounting of the european budget, and new standards will be introduced for this part of the budgetary accounting which will be in line with the international standards which have recently been adopted - i will come to that in a moment. for the implementation of the new standards in accordance with the financial regulation from 2005, the commission's accounting officer will develop the concrete details of how to implement these international standards for the respective actions financed by the budget of the european union. a new accounting standards committee will be set up to this end. the committee will include various experts from the services, but also external experts. it is also important that the new system applies not only to the accounting, to the accounting of the commission, but to all the european institutions, that is to say also to the european parliament, to the budget of the council or the court of auditors and also to the agencies. the relevant standards are indeed very new. the first public sector standard was adopted by this international body in 2000 and the last in october 2002. so these are completely new regulations that we are using as a benchmark for our accounting system. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the implementation of the new standards also requires the modernisation of the technical support provided by the underlying computer system, i.e. the information sector. the current system for accounting is the sincom-ii system. it was introduced in 1997 and extended to all smes in 1999. the commission's accounting system is based on the sap system - or, in short, the a3 system for all experts - and the closure of the books and the presentation of the annual accounts are based entirely on this system. the si-ii system was developed for intermediate management bodies, which requires the entry of data on all relevant measures, i.e. all data relevant to accounting. experience shows, however, that the future system must be a fully integrated system from an accounting point of view. however, to the extent that full integration allows, the different needs of the services should also be taken into account and the corresponding management tasks should be met, which are also subject to different requirements for the various sections of the european budget. the modernisation of the it system is based on three options: to continue with the current system, to introduce a completely new system or to develop an integrated system from the existing one. the first option - you will have noticed from my words - is rejected by the commission. for the second and third options, a feasibility study will be carried out in the first half of 2003. the decision is then made as to what further steps can be taken to develop an integrated system. what is the project organization like, because this project is a comprehensive project for the commission and also for the whole of the european institutions. the project organisation places the accounting officer at the centre of the project. in order to achieve this ambitious reform, the commission has also decided today to appoint mr brian gray, the new deputy director-general of the dg for budgets, as its accounting officer. the commission has thus decided to assign the highest grade, a1, to this project, to make it clear that the necessary authority is also necessary from the grade to bring all the institutions and all the services on board. a project oversight board will be set up, with representatives from the main services and also from the european court of auditors, to support the implementation of this ambitious and complex project. with mr gray as accounting officer and mr oostens as director of the accounting department, two qualified, experienced accountants and qualified managers are at the head of this ambitious project which, if it succeeds - and i am confident it will - will bring the eu administration to the forefront of modern public administration, also from the accounting side. overall, the package of decisions taken today underlines that the commission has now decided that the commission and the reform package for administration have long since moved beyond the concept stage, into implementation, and i am pleased to have been able to present today to the european parliament, which is supporting us and following us critically, these advances in financial management.
- mr president, i have a few questions for the commissioner. i am of course glad that a report is now being produced, but i must say that there is a very big discrepancy between the commission's report and what the court of auditors says. it would be interesting to have a few comments on this. the report also states that there are serious problems with the system - we know this from the court of auditors and from a certain mrs andreasen. the system's inability to talk to each other is also a very big weakness. i am glad that the possibility of fraud and irregularities is being rejected, but the commission claims that too few authorized users have access to the system, and the court of auditors disagrees. i would like to know why there is such a great disagreement between the commission and the court of auditors. there are some questions we really want to hear about in the committee on budgetary control.
commission. – madam president, it was nearly two years ago that the commission adopted its proposal for a directive establishing quality and safety requirements for blood and blood components. this was presented to the council and parliament in january 2001. considerable work has been done since then, and i welcome the constructive way in which the proposal has been addressed both in parliament and in the council. parliament has now reached a major milestone in its consideration of this proposal. your pending approval of this proposed directive is the culmination of considerable efforts on the part of the council in reaching its common position, taking on many of parliament’s amendments from first reading; on the part of the committee on the environment, public health and consumer policy in proposing amendments to that position and on the part of parliament's delegation in reaching a constructive compromise with the council. may i express my sincere thanks to mr nisticò and mr lund in particular for their strenuous efforts in seeing this technically complex directive through to a successful conclusion in such a short time and for finding a compromise on the issue of voluntary unpaid donations. i very much hope that the commission’s proposal for a directive on tissues and cells will be considered with the same interest and efficiency.
- thank you to mr nisticò and commissioner byrne for a great deal of work on this case. the result of the conciliation will mean that we now have a high minimum level of safety and quality of blood and blood components for transfusion. the requirements are the qualifications of the responsible staff, the operation of the blood banks, the testing of donor blood and also effective control and monitoring. the most debated issue has been whether blood should be donated without a real payment to the donor. i mean, it's well documented that voluntary and unpaid donation gives the best blood quality, especially if you have a regular donor body. but the different rules in the member states have meant that we have not been able to decide that free donation should apply in all member states from day one. nevertheless, i am very pleased that the directive now refers to the council of europe's correct definition of blood donation free of charge. i am also very pleased that the member states are now taking measures to encourage voluntary blood donation and that they are reporting every three years to the commission on the measures they have taken. finally, i think it is important that each member state has the right to maintain and introduce voluntary and unpaid blood donation requirements, a requirement which also includes a ban on the import of blood and blood components which have not been donated in accordance with these principles of unpaid donation. i certainly believe that the directive we have reached here will make an important contribution to the safety and quality of blood donation in europe.
- madam president, a big thank you to mrs ries for a great job on this. it is a good example of how the european parliament can help to promote the protection of the environment and health in europe. the commission simply prohibited the marketing and use of one of the dangerous flame retardants, pentabromodiphenyl ether, and, at the request of parliament, succeeded in extending the directive to include two additional flame retardants in the final outcome of the conciliation committee. as regards octabromodiphenyl ether, the risk assessment has clearly revealed that the substance poses clear risks to the environment and human health and there is now also agreement to implement an immediate ban on the use of this substance, and that is good. as regards the third substance, decabromodiphenyl ether, the member states and the commission have, however, only recently maintained their opposition to a ban. a majority in the conciliation committee chose to accept that the substance is included in one recital in the directive, has been given a yellow card, if i may say so, as quoted by mrs ries. it can be said that the environment and human health will now bear the risk of the use of this substance for some time to come. i think that is very unfortunate, and it illustrates the importance of the fact that in the chemical sector we can reverse the burden of proof, so that it is the producers who have to prove that the substances they use are safe for the environment and for humans. but unlike mrs schörling, i believe that the result, the adoption of the compromise result, is after all a clear improvement on the situation today. so i will vote for the compromise motion and the compromise result in the final vote tomorrow.
madam president, honor where honor's due. anders fogh has done much better than the euro. the danish prime minister and the danish ambassador to the european union deserve praise for having concluded the negotiations in time, but the result is a disaster. never before have so many rich countries been so stingy with so few. the majority of polish farmers will become net contributors to the eu. they will pay more in eu quota than they get. improved agriculture, on the other hand, will see a large increase in wealth, which will be paid back to future farmers. the cost of an acre of farmland in the netherlands is 30 times that in poland. giving polish and dutch farmers the same right to buy land from each other does not mean equality! the agricultural systems must now be radically revised to avoid the capitalisation of higher agricultural prices leading to new support needs and the sending of one million polish farmers to an unemployed life in the cities. budgetary and structural funds should also be reviewed. cut off subsidies to rich countries! the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. let us make a drastic cut in the 85,000 pages of eu law! let us only demand that the applicant countries comply with our standards when they export to us. let's give them longer transitions, more ropes, more freedom. let us allow for much greater flexibility than what has now been negotiated in the field. i'm not criticizing anders fogh for the result. he could not have done much better with the mandate he had been given, but now, as prime minister of denmark, he should say that enlargement will fail without radical reforms. in a union of 25 countries and more on the way, you cannot legislate as if it were a state. the eu should now focus on the cross-border aspects and let the member states' parliaments adopt the bulk of the laws. an enlarged union can only function as a europe of democracies and diversity. we saw that coming when we named our group.

as it often does - it cannot overshadow the big picture. we simply cannot hand over to the next generations a more secure and democratic foundation than the strong community that has been created here. we must also be clear that when the new year celebrations are over, we must get our feet back on the ground, and there are at least two very big tasks ahead of us, and also of the new countries. one is to ensure that eu legislation is effectively implemented in the new countries, that it is implemented administratively and legally in practice, so that union standards are actually respected. the second is agricultural, and the way in which the budgetary compromise was created may not have made it easier to envisage a necessary and real agricultural reform. we all know that the present agricultural policy is grotesque and very harmful to the developing countries. i think that this is a field in which political courage and responsibility will be needed in the future. finally, a few words about turkey: turkey has already made two decisions which i think are very important. one is that it is a candidate country which can join on the same terms as other candidate countries. secondly, if the commission can establish in december 2004 that the criteria - democracy, the rule of law, respect for human rights or the protection of minorities - are in place in turkey, so that more reforms are really needed and, above all, that the reforms must be implemented. but it was a wise decision taken at the european council last week on turkey. once again, thank you for a great presidency and congratulations on the result.

- mr president, mr president-in-office, mr prime minister, congratulations on the fantastic results achieved by you, the danish government and the danish civil service. the result, a united europe, belongs to us all, but your skillful planning and political will were decisive. with enlargement, the second world war is finally over. this was made clear in the polish press room on friday. poland was one of the hardest hit areas in europe in the last century, battered from both east and west by regimes whose totalitarian ideologies left only destruction and horrible memories. the past inequalities have been eliminated, europe is reunited, the word solidarity has acquired a new meaning, europe is healed. the european parliament has been a great help to the commission in its efforts to bring about a change in the legal system. the eu is thus the world's most binding and the only democratic cooperation between independent countries. we should be proud of that, and we are proud of that. but this unique partnership requires much of us. our citizens rightly expect results and solutions, we must be able to make decisions and be effective. we must therefore focus on the essentials while preserving our diversity. the council must take decisions by qualified majority, which means a willingness to compromise. the european parliament must be an equal co-legislator and when the council legislates we must all be able to follow. the law, not brute force, is what counts in the eu. the alpha and omega is therefore that our common eu rules are respected by everyone, big and small, in the north and in the south, in the east and in the west. this can only be done with a strong commission. an enlarged union requires that we all safeguard the values on which the community is founded. that was the message that the summit sent to turkey. turkey still has a lot to do and must get back to work. the country will be thoroughly assessed in 2004 and then, if it meets the copenhagen criteria, will be given a date for the start of accession negotiations. turkey will thus be treated in exactly the same way as all the other candidate countries - because in the eu the law prevails and not the power. the copenhagen summit shows that the european union is a great vision and a historic decision, but at the same time a time-consuming and sometimes unpleasant negotiation on milk quotas, suckler cows and all sorts of transitional arrangements. we politicians must be able to explain both sides of this wonderful european cooperation. i would like to thank the president and congratulate him once again - you have earned the right to celebrate, and not just with a beer.
- madam president, unfortunately this is not the first time that israel has vandalized religious and cultural monuments and violated the venice charter and all the conventions of unesco and icomos without the world protesting. this time the world should protest as loudly as it did in the barbaric destruction of the buddha statues in afghanistan. it is an irreplaceable heritage of religious and cultural buildings from the mamluk period in the 15th century to the ottoman period in the 19th century. cultural values that belong not only to the palestinians, but to the whole world. destroying these cultural values has nothing to do with security or self-defence. it is pure revenge and a revenge that will continue the spiral of violence in which israeli soldiers and settlers kill palestinian civilians with tanks and guns in total secrecy and their young relatives blow themselves up under the full cover of the media. over 300 homes are facing destruction. the historic city of hebron, with its unique architecture and religious significance, will never be the same again. it is a disgrace and the eu must call on the world community to take responsibility for stopping it.
